- `ValidateLightColor` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c2df0, 672 bytes): **established.**
  Sends one light's three colours. It selects register 0x29 index 0x19a (stored at +0xa1f8), calls `__glUnScaleColorf` three times to convert the light state's diffuse (+0x10), specular (+0x20) and ambient (+0x00) colours into the 48-byte per-slot shadow at +0xa034 + 48 x slot, and sends each as a select of register 0x32 index 3 x slot + 1 (diffuse), + 2 (specular) and + 0 (ambient) followed by `0x8e809015` and four floats. When the byte at +0xc94 is zero (a colour-index visual) the first float of the diffuse and specular colour is first replaced by the luminance 0.59 g + 0.30 r + 0.11 b (constants 0x3fe2e147ae147ae1, 0x3fd3333333333333, 0x3fbc28f5c28f5c29), and the first float of the ambient colour becomes minus the product of those two luminances.
- `ValidateFragmentLightColor` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c3090, 1008 bytes): **established.**
  Packs a fragment light's ambient, diffuse and specular colours into the six words of one light's fixed-point record and sends them. It calls `__glUnScaleColorf` for ambient (from the light state itself), diffuse (+0x10) and specular (+0x20) into the shadow at +0xa034 + 48 x slot, then turns each float into a 12-bit field: 4095 when the value is 1.0 or more, otherwise the value times 4096, truncated. Two fields share a word, the first in bits 23:12 and the second in bits 11:0: word 0 holds ambient r and g, word 1 holds ambient b in bits 23:12, words 2 and 3 are the same for diffuse and words 4 and 5 for specular; the low bits of words 1 and 3 are set to 10 (bits 11:10) and word 5 gets 1010 in bits 11:8. The six words live at +0x9754 + 24 x n for light n and go out after the settle sequence (when the flag at +0x9830 is set) as `0x10407` with operand n, then the class-1 block 0x250 + n with six operands.
- `__glOdysseyValidateLighting` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c3480, 1620 bytes): **established** for the scene colour and the ordinary-light loop, **inferred** for the fragment-lighting slot (the tag handling after the loop was read as a flow but not every guard was traced to a GL state name).
  Brings the board's light table up to date. It first sends the scene (light-model) ambient: converted with `__glUnScaleColorf` from +0x574 into +0xa1b4 for an RGBA visual, or the fixed (1, 0, 0, 1) for a colour-index visual, then register 0x29 index 0x19a, register 0x32 index 24, `0x8e809015` and four floats. It sets bit 11 of the word at +0x7bfc when the byte at +0x585 (two-sided lighting, named from the layout of the public sample's light-model state) and bit 6 of the enable word at +0x700 (lighting) are both set, and raises dirty bit 2 of +0x16960. With lighting enabled, it walks the eight lights by the mask at +0x708 and packs the enabled ones into consecutive slots k: a slot is rewritten when its tag at +0x9734 + 4k differs from 0x4000 + n or the light's bit is set in the change word +0x16968 (position, calling `ValidateLight` with f16 = 0) or +0x1696c (colour, calling `ValidateLightColor`), and the tag becomes 0x4000 + n. The light state records are 116 bytes apart from the pointer at +0x630. When bit 12 of the second enable word +0x704 is set (fragment lighting) and bit 0 of the word at +0x1647c is set, the next slot takes the fragment-lighting light at +0x16480 with tag 0x840c: `ValidateLight` with f16 = 3.0 for slots k and 8, `ValidateLightColor` on a static default record at 0xdaec858 and `ValidateFragmentLightColor` with n = 0; bit 0 of +0x16970 is cleared. When lighting is off but fragment lighting is on, one slot takes tag 9998 by the same calls. Every other slot below 8 with a tag other than 9999 is overwritten with the default record and tagged 9999. It ends by restoring the saved register 0x29 index at +0xa1f8 and sending selects of register 0x29, 0x32 (index 0) and 0x31 (index 0). Re-read against the whole body (405 instructions): it also stores 2 into the begin-mode word at absolute address 4120 at 0xd9c35d4, in the lighting-enabled path just after raising dirty bit 2 of +0x16960. Left inferred because the paths that reach the fragment-lighting block and the tag-9998 block pass a pointer through the stack words 0(sp) and 48(sp) that some paths read before the routine has stored them: 0(sp) is read at 0xd9c361c and 0xd9c3974 and 48(sp) at 0xd9c3710, 0xd9c3954 and 0xd9c39ac, and when no ordinary light is enabled (or lighting is off but fragment lighting is on) no store to either word has run on the way. What the callee `ValidateLight` does with that pointer when its fourth argument is 0 was not settled, so which of those uses read a meaningless value is open.
- `__glOdysseyInitLighting` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c2230, 856 bytes): **established.**
  Gives the board the GL initial lighting state when a context is created. It marks all eight lights dirty (+0x16968 and +0x1696c = 0xff), sends the ten attribute words listed in `docs/tokens.md` ("Material slots 6 to 13"), zeroes the two four-word slots at indices 0xf0 and 0xf4 of register 0x31 (header `0x908091bb`, the header the clip-plane sender uses) and sets the scene ambient: 0.2, 0.2, 0.2, 1.0 at +0xa1b4 to +0xa1c0 for an RGBA visual (byte +0xc94 set) and 1, 0, 0, 1 for a colour-index visual, sent as register 0x29 index 0x19a, register 0x32 index 0x18, `0x8e809015` and the four floats. It then restores +0xa1f8 to its old value, sets +0xa21c to 0, +0xa1d4 to 3, +0xa218 to 0 and +0xa1d8 to 0x10, and sends selects of register 0x29, 0x32 (index 0), 0x20 (index 3), 0x31 (index 0) and 0x21 (index 0x10).
- `__glOdysseyComputeLightState` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9f0bc0, 764 bytes): **established.**
  Rebuilds the software lighting records for the enabled lights, used by the soft-clip path. For each light n below the count at +0xe04 whose bit is set in +0x708 it links the next 224-byte record from the array at +0x6df8 onto the list head at +0x6e4c, points it at the state (+0x60), copies the eye position from state +0x40 to +0x4c into +0x74 to +0x80, copies the attenuation terms (+0x64 to +0x6c), sets the spot flag at +0x9c when the cutoff differs from 180 and stores its cosine (cutoff x 0.017453292) at +0x94. For a spot light whose exponent changed (compared at +0x70) it frees the cached table with `__glFreeSpecLUT` and fetches a new one with `__glCreateSpecLUT`, copying the table pointer, threshold and scale into +0xcc, +0xd0 and +0xd4 and the cache pointer into +0xd8. When the constant attenuation is nonzero it keeps the reciprocal-style factor at +0x98 (the constant at +0xdd8 divided by it). For a colour-index visual (byte +0xc95) it stores the luminances of the specular and diffuse colours at +0xc0 and +0xc4. With a local viewer (byte +0x584) the record's slow-path byte +0xdc is 1; otherwise a directional, non-spot light gets its unit light vector at +0xb0 and half-way vector at +0xa0 through the normalising callback at +0x2eb8 of the context, and +0xdc is 0. The list ends with a NULL pointer.
- `__glOdysseyValidateFragment` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9821a0, 760 bytes): **established.**
  Sets the board's fragment-lighting controls from the lighting enables. It decides whether separate specular colour must be made by the board: the colour-control word at +0x588 is 0x81fa (separate specular), lighting (bit 6 of +0x700) is on with an RGBA visual and fragment lighting (bit 12 of +0x704) is off, at least one of the enabled lights below the count at +0xe04 exists, and either bit 30 of +0x700 or bit 16 or 17 is set. In that case bit 19 of +0x7c00 is set and the six packed words of light 0 at +0x9754 are rewritten to ambient and diffuse 0 and specular 4095 (so light 0 passes only the specular term), then sent as `0x10407` operand 0 and block 0x250 with the settle sequence first when the flag +0x9830 is set; bit 0 of +0x16970 is set. Bits 15:13 of +0x7c00 become 2 when fragment lighting or the separate-specular case is active with an RGBA visual and 1 otherwise. Bits 26:24 of the word at +0x9580 take the light-environment mode stored at +0x165b0: 3 for GL_ADD (0x104), 1 for GL_REPLACE (0x1e01), 2 for GL_MODULATE (0x2100), and 3 when fragment lighting is off. It raises dirty bits 23 and 2 of +0x16960, stores 2 in the global at 0x1018 and calls `ValidateFragmentColorMaterial`.
- `ValidateFragmentColorMaterial` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd981fc0, 468 bytes): **established.**
  Rebuilds the two material-source words at +0x9580 and +0x9584 (front and back) from the fragment colour-material state. Each word holds four 2-bit fields, ambient in bits 1:0, diffuse 3:2, specular 5:4 and emission 7:6, and every field starts as 2 (take the material). When bit 13 of +0x704 is set it reads the face at +0x165a8 and the parameter at +0x165ac: for a face of GL_FRONT (0x404) or GL_FRONT_AND_BACK (0x408) the front word, and for GL_BACK (0x405) or GL_FRONT_AND_BACK the back word, has the field of the named parameter set to 3 (take the current colour): 0x1200 ambient, 0x1201 diffuse, 0x1202 specular, 0x1600 emission, 0x1602 ambient and diffuse together. It then raises dirty bit 23 of +0x16960 and stores 2 in the global at 0x1018.
- `__glOdysseyInitFragmentLighting` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9851c0, 80 bytes): **established.**
  Puts the fragment-lighting state of a new context at the GL initial values. It sets the colour-material face at +0x165a8 to 0x408 and its parameter at +0x165ac to 0x1602, sets bit 0 of +0x16970, and writes into the fragment light record at +0x16480 the floats 1.0 at +0x164b8 (position z), 1.0 at +0x164c8 (eye position z), -1.0 at +0x164d8 (spot direction z), 180.0 at +0x164e4 (cutoff) and 1.0 at +0x164e8 (constant attenuation); no other word of the record is written.

## Soft-clip planes, software lighting and the soft-clip triangle

- `__glOdysseyValidateSoftClipPlanes` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9ee360, 1724 bytes): **established.**
  Builds the clip planes the host has to apply itself, the user planes beyond what the board holds. It calls `__glOdysseyGetNumHWClipPlanes`, copies the 4 x 4 matrix record that +0x7414 points to onto the stack as its transpose, and clears the valid-plane mask at +0x16818. It walks the 13 plane slots by the change word +0x70c: the first as many enabled planes as the board holds are skipped, and each later enabled plane (the four floats at 16 x slot in the plane array that the word at +0x6d4 points to) is multiplied by the transposed matrix with `__glMipsXForm4` into the software plane at +0x16748 + 16 x slot, setting its bit in +0x16818. When at least one software plane exists and the projection class at +0x19dbc is nonzero it adds more planes: slot 8 of the user array is set to (0, 0, s, s x n) with s = -1 when n is below f and 1 otherwise (n and f are the doubles at +0x165c8 and +0x165d0), or to (0, 0, -1, 1) when the byte at +0x19dcd is set; for class 3 slots 9 to 12 are built from the matrix at +0x741c as row 3 plus or minus 0.9 times row 0 (slots 9 and 10) and row 3 plus or minus 0.9 times row 1 (slots 11 and 12), a guard band at 0.9 of the view volume (constant 0x3feccccccccccccd), and bits 8, then 9 to 12, are added to the mask. Slots 8 to 12 are then transformed like the others. It ends by clearing the byte at +0x19dc8. The test that skips all of this when the count of enabled planes in slots 0 to 5 equals the number of unused board planes was read as written; what the board planes cover is **inferred** from `__glOdysseyValidateClipPlanes`.
- `__glOdysseySoftClipCalcRGBColor` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9f0fa0, 2220 bytes): **established.**
  Lights one vertex in RGBA mode on the host: arguments are the context, a face number (0 front, nonzero back) and the vertex record. It reads the normal at vertex +0x20 to +0x28 (negated for the back face) and writes the lit colour to +0x90 (front) or +0xa0 (back) and the specular colour to +0x170 or +0x180. It re-applies five `__glim_Materialfv` calls (ambient 0x1200, diffuse 0x1201, specular 0x1202, emission 0x1600, shininess 0x1601) for the face, with bit 7 of +0x700 (colour material) cleared during the calls, when the vertex's byte at +0x190 is zero (taking the material block at vertex +0xd0 for the front face and +0x120 for the back) or when that byte is set and the cache byte at +0x19d8c (front) or +0x19d8d (back) is clear (taking the context's copy at +0x1681c or +0x1686c); the cache byte then records whether the vertex byte was nonzero. It then walks the light list from +0x6e4c (each record's next pointer at +0xc8), builds the unit vector to the light and, for a local viewer (byte +0x584) or a local light, the half-way vector, through the normaliser at +0x2eb8 of the context; the attenuation is 1 / (k0 + k1 d + k2 d squared) with d the distance, or the cached factor at +0x98 when k1 and k2 are zero; a spot light multiplies it by an entry of its 256-entry table indexed from (cosine - threshold) x scale when the cosine passes the cutoff (+0x94). Diffuse adds n.L times the light's diffuse product, specular adds the specular-table value of n.h times the specular product, scaled by the attenuation, summed with the scene colour of the material machine (+0x6dfc front, +0x6e24 back). With the colour control at +0x588 equal to 0x81fa and any of bits 30, 17 or 16 of +0x700 set, the specular sum goes to the separate specular colour; otherwise it is added to the primary colour. Each component is clamped to 0 to 1 (the float at +0xdd8) and alpha comes from the material machine at +0x24. The structure is that of the public sample implementation's `__glCalcRGBColor` (`gfx/lib/opengl/glcore/s_light.c`); the reading here is of this binary's body.
- `__glOdysseySoftClipCalcCIColor` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9f1850, 1564 bytes): **established.**
  Lights one vertex in colour-index mode on the host, with the same arguments and light loop as the RGBA routine but accumulating two scalars. For each lit light the spot and attenuation factor f is formed as above; the diffuse sum d gains the light's diffuse luminance (record +0xc4) times n.L times f, and the specular sum s gains the specular luminance (+0xc0) times the specular-table value times f. The result is cmapa + d (1 - min(s, 1)) (cmapd - cmapa) + min(s, 1) (cmaps - cmapa), where cmapa, cmaps and cmapd are the material's three colour-index floats at +0x44, +0x48 and +0x4c, limited to cmaps, and is stored at vertex +0x90 or +0xa0. The two luminance fields are those `__glOdysseyComputeLightState` stores for a colour-index visual.
- `__glOdysseySoftClipRenderTriangleFast` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9ea840, 3852 bytes): **established.**
  Sends one already-clipped triangle to the board as three vertex records. It writes the primitive-begin word `0x14400` with operand 4 (the value of GL_TRIANGLES; inferred to be the primitive type), then for each vertex the attribute words of the groups enabled in the word at +0x168c0: bit 0x20 and bit 0x10 the back and front material groups (`0xca80d035`, `0xcc80d037`, `0xcb80d036`, `0xcd80d038` and `0xc680d02f`, `0xc880d031`, `0xc780d030`, `0xc980d032`, four floats each, from the context's material shadow at +0x1681c (front) and +0x1686c (back) or from the vertex's own copy at +0xd0 and +0x120, the shadow being used when the vertex's flag byte at +0x190 and the face's dirty byte are set), bit 1 the normal `0xc180cc19` with three floats from vertex +0xc0, bit 4 the colour `0xc580d015` with four floats from +0x90, bit 2 the texture coordinate `0xc380d025` with four floats from +0x30, and always the position `0x8080d005` with four floats from +0x10. With flat shading (the shade model at +0x570 is not 0x1d01) the second and third vertices send only texture coordinate and position. When the context's material shadow was used it calls `__glOdyim_Materialfv` with GL_SHININESS (0x1601, written as 5633 in the code) for the front or back face and clears the matching dirty byte at +0x168c4 or +0x168c5. It ends with the primitive-end word `0x14001`, the settle prefix `0x10443`, 250, `0x10046` twice when the word at +0x96f4 is set (the `lw` at 0xd9eac7c, tested by the `beq` at 0xd9eac80), and sets the settle-needed word +0x9830 to 1. The slot-to-field pairing of the material words supports the reading in `docs/tokens.md` that slots 6 to 9 are ambient, diffuse, specular and emission.

## Texture generation and small texture setters

- `__glOdysseyValidateTexGen` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9cf3e0, 324 bytes): **established.**
  Re-sends the texture-coordinate generation planes that changed. It tests bits 18 to 21 of the enable word at +0x700 and, for each set bit, calls `updateTexGenCoord` with the generator record at +0x7c0, +0x83c, +0x8b8 or +0x934 (124 bytes apart) and the slot number 256, 260, 264 or 268. It saves the register-0x31 table index at +0xa218 on entry, and when a call changed it writes it back and sends `0x31008000 | enc(index)` so the next table write lands where it did before.
- `updateTexGenCoord` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9cf240, 408 bytes): **established.**
  Sends one texture-generation plane to the board. The record's mode word at +0 must be GL_EYE_LINEAR (0x2400) or GL_OBJECT_LINEAR (0x2401); any other mode returns without sending. For the eye mode the four floats at +4 to +0x10 are multiplied by the 4 x 4 matrix record that +0x7414 points to (output i is the sum of plane float j times matrix element 4 i + j); for the object mode the four floats at +0x14 are used unchanged. The result is kept at +0x9834 + 4 x slot, then sent as a register-0x31 select of index slot (stored at +0xa218) followed by `0x908091bb` and the four floats.
- `__glOdysseyTextureSetMaxLOD` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9d5de0, 96 bytes): **established.**
  Derives the object's maximum level-of-detail field. It takes the smaller of (the last level at +0x60 minus the base level at +0x5c) and the float at +0x58, limits it to -15 through 15, multiplies by 256, truncates and keeps the low 14 bits in the word at +0xcc, and sets bit 14 of the dirty word at +0xa0.
- `__glOdysseyTextureSetRWrap` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9d5d20, 192 bytes): **established.**
  Applies a GL_TEXTURE_WRAP_R value to a texture object. It stores the argument at +0x24; the effective mode at +0x28 is the argument for a 3D target (the target at +0xc is 0x806f) and GL_REPEAT otherwise. GL_REPEAT (0x2901) gives codes 0 and 0, GL_CLAMP (0x2900) 2 and 0, GL_CLAMP_TO_EDGE (0x812f) 1 and 1, and GL_CLAMP_TO_BORDER (0x812d) 3 and 63; any other value tail-calls `__glSetError` with GL_INVALID_ENUM (0x500). The first code goes into bits 9:8 of the word at +0xb4 and the second into bits 23:18 of the word at +0xb0, and bit 9 of the dirty word at +0xa0 is set.
- `SetTexOffsetValues` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9d2660, 108 bytes): **established.**
  Resets a texture object's three offset words at +0xd0, +0xd4 and +0xd8 to zero and sets two mirrored flag bits from the word at +0x57c of the object: when it is nonzero bit 24 of the word at +0xb4 is cleared and bit 20 of the word at +0xbc is set, and when it is zero the opposite. What the word at +0x57c records is unknown; its writers were not traced.
- `SetDetailEntry` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9d84c0, 196 bytes): **established.**
  Writes one detail-texture scale into a word table. The float argument is limited to 0 through 1 and stored in bits 23:12 of table word `index` as an unsigned 12-bit fraction (4095 for 1.0, otherwise the value times 4096, truncated); bits 25 and 27 of that word are cleared and every other bit is kept.

## Flush, swap, draw-buffer select and clear

The routines below end in the same bracket that the validator's bit 21 group sends. Call it the descriptor bracket: the settle sequence, then either the word `0x90008116` or, when bit 27 of the descriptor word at +0x96a4 is set, three blocks `0x041` (operands 0, 0), `0x04b` (eight operands: 0, 0x00ffffff and six zeros) and `0x040` (0, 0), then `0x11017` with the four words at +0x969c to +0x96a8. The word `0x10a5a` is class-1 block 0x25a with two operands.

- `__glOdysseyFinish` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9d9800, 788 bytes): **established.**
  Waits until the board has drained everything sent so far. It sends the settle sequence twice, then the descriptor bracket; resends the `0x11017` group with its third word zero and again with bit 23 of the third word set (the descriptor word at +0x96a4); sends the settle sequence and the bracket once more, clearing the settle-needed word +0x9830 on the way. It then increments the 16-bit counter at +0xa6b4, sends `0x10a5a` with two operands, the word at +0x16d3c and the new counter, and spins in user space reading the 16-bit slot at the pointer in +0x16c98 plus twice the word at +0x16d3c until it equals the counter (it also makes one dummy load from the object that +0x16c6c points to). The pointer and the slot word are the values `__glOdysseyInitKernel` stores from the kernel's reply, so the board is expected to write the counter back to a slot the kernel mapped; the board's side of that write is **inferred**.
- `__glOdysseySwapBuffers` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9d9450, 840 bytes): **established.**
  Asks for a buffer swap on a double-buffered visual and does nothing when the byte at +0xc96 is zero. It sends the settle sequence, increments the counter at +0xa6b4 and sends `0x10a5a` with the word at +0x16d3c OR 0x8000 and the counter (the same marker `__glOdysseyFinish` waits on, with bit 15 set), then settle sequences and two descriptor brackets, and calls `__glOdysseyIoctl` with request 1005 and a three-word argument whose first word is the word at +0x7b4c. A negative result is reported through the callback at +0x14. It does not wait.
- `__glOdysseySetDrawbuf` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9d9fb0, 808 bytes): **established.**
  Selects which of four buffers are drawn to. The four arguments after the context are each tested for nonzero and combined into a mask (1, 2, 4 and 8); the mask is sent with `__glOdysseyIoctl` request 0x6f640009, a nonzero result is reported through the callback at +0x14, and the board is then resynchronised: the settle sequence, two descriptor brackets (the second preceded by `0x11017` groups with the third word zero and with bit 23 set) and a closing `0x10443` 250 `0x10046` `0x10046`, with the settle-needed word +0x9830 cleared.
- `__glOdysseyClear` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd964aa0, 1996 bytes): **established.**
  Clears a window rectangle with one colour (and a depth value) by turning the pipeline into a flat fill. The arguments are the context, a pointer to four colour floats, a write-mask byte, the rectangle as x, y, width, height, one more flag and the depth as a double. It saves the words at +0x7bfc, +0x7c00, +0x7c04, +0x7c2c, +0x7c30 and +0x96a4 and rewrites them: in +0x7bfc bits 2, 4, 7, 8, 11, 14, 17, 19, 20 and 22 are cleared and bits 12, 15 and 16 set; in +0x7c00 bits 5:0 and 11:10 are cleared and bits 15:13 set to 1; +0x7c04 gets 1010 in bits 3:0; +0x7c2c gets the write-mask byte in bits 7:0 and 0xff in bits 15:8; +0x7c30 gets bits 2:0 set. It sends blocks `0x10420`, `0x1041f` and `0x10415` with the new words (the settle prefix first when +0x9830 is set), raises dirty bits 1, 2 and 21 of +0x16960 and calls `__glOdysseyValidateHardware`. A fill through the kernel is chosen when bit 4 of +0x7b70 is set, the area width x height is not below the word at +0x19dd4, bit 27 of +0x96a4 is clear and three further tests on the write masks and the descriptor word at +0x96a4 pass; it then calls `__glOdysseyIoctl` with request 0x6f64000e and a 0x30-byte argument (the clear colour, the depth and the rectangle's corners). If that call fails, or the kernel path was not chosen, the routine draws the fill itself: `0x14400` with operand 7, the state word `0x8f80a944` with the four colour floats, two corner positions as x, y, z triples (z is the double argument as a float) and `0x14001`, followed by the settle prefix when the word at +0x96f4 is set and the settle-needed word +0x9830 set to 1. It finishes by restoring every saved word, raising dirty bits 1, 2 and 21 again and resending `0x10415`, `0x10420` and `0x1041f`. What bits of the three state words mean to the board is unknown; the routes are the block lists in `docs/buzz.md` (the second three-layout set) and the per-word search in `docs/tokens.md`.

## Fast-dispatch set-up and current-value copy

- `__glOdyssey_FastDispatchEnd` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xdadc200, 15872 bytes): **established.**
  Not a routine: 3968 `nop` instructions that pad the library's code to the end of its page, and the label that marks the end of the fast-dispatch code. `InitFastDispatch` subtracts the address of `glColor3b`, the first of the generated entry points, from this label to get the length of the region it remaps. All 3968 instructions of the listing in `libglcore/asm/ODYSSEY.IP30.N32_M4.O/g_ody_api.s` were checked; there is no other opcode in the range.
- `InitFastDispatch` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd967420, 504 bytes): **established.**
  Makes the library's fast-dispatch code writable in the process, once. It allocates a 56-byte record with the host callback at +0 of its argument (kept at the fixed process address 0x17b4) holding the region length (`__glOdyssey_FastDispatchEnd` minus `glColor3b`) and start. The first caller to win `__glOdysseyAtomicTestAndSet` on gp-13608 allocates a private copy of the region and copies the code into it with `memcpy`; the others wait with `sginap(0)` until the flag at gp-13612 is set. Each then opens `/dev/zero` read-write, maps it over the region with `mmap` (protection 7, flags 146, fixed address), checks that the mapping landed at the region's start, closes the file, copies the saved code back, and fills the record's following words (+8 = -1, +0xc = 5120, +0x18 = 1280, +0x20 = 4096, +0x28 = 6144, the rest 0). It returns 1 on success and 0 on any failure and clears the word at 0x17b8 first.
- `setCurrent` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd970bd0, 340 bytes): **established.**
  Replays a saved set of current vertex values through the context's own entry points. From the record argument it calls the entry at +128 of the table at +0x17f4 twelve times, the front face (0x404) then the back face (0x405) for ambient 0x1200 at +0x34 and +0x84, diffuse 0x1201 at +0x44 and +0x94, specular 0x1202 at +0x54 and +0xa4, emission 0x1600 at +0x64 and +0xb4, shininess 0x1601 at +0x74 and +0xc4 and colour indexes 0x1603 at +0x78 and +0xc8; then it calls the table entries at +1500, +1556, +1604 and +1732 with the record at +0, +0x10, +0x14 and +0x20 and tail-calls the entry at +0x28 with +0x30. The entries are named from the argument widths: material, colour 4fv, index, normal 3fv, texture coordinate and edge flag (**inferred**).
- `getCurrent` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9704c0, 1800 bytes): **established.**
  Fills the record that `setCurrent` replays (arguments: a0 the context, a1 the record; no return value is set, v0 holds whatever the last path left). The record layout it writes: current colour at +0, current index at +16, normal at +20, texture coordinate at +32, edge flag byte at +48, then 40 material floats at +52 to +208 (front face words +52 to +128, back face words +132 to +208). It loads the get entry at +496 of the dispatch table at context +0x17f4 (6132) and compares it with `__glOdyim_SoftGetFloatv` (the local GOT entry 869, 0xd9f3ef0). Software path, when they are equal. When bit 7 of the enable word at +0x700 (colour material) is set it stores 0 into the word at +0x1697c (in the delay slot of the call) and calls `__glOdyim_Enable` (GOT slot at gp-32496) with 0xb57; when the bit is clear and the word at +0x1697c is nonzero it calls `__glOdyim_Disable` (gp-32368) with 0xb57; otherwise neither call. So the routine re-issues the colour-material enable or disable, it does not turn colour material off. It then calls the get entry at +496 with 0xb00 into +0, 0xb01 into +16, 0xb02 into +20 and 0xb03 into +32, the get entry at +480 with 0xb43 into +48, and the material entry at +524 twelve times, face 0x404 then face 0x405, each with the parameters 0x1200, 0x1201, 0x1202, 0x1600, 0x1601 and 0x1603 into +52, +68, +84, +100, +116 and +120 for the front face and +132, +148, +164, +180, +196 and +200 for the back face. Hardware path, when they differ. If the byte at context +0x19d8e is zero it first calls `__glOdysseyGetHardwareShadow` (0xd9d93e0, GOT slot at gp-30512) with the context and 0. It then calls `bcopy` (global GOT slot at gp-16968) with source the word at +0x16d28 (stored by `__glOdysseyInitKernel`), destination the frame (sp) and length 2304 (0x900). It decodes four words with `ConvertFromShadow` (0xd96fce0), each call taking the context, a destination, a field of the copied shadow masked with 0x3ff and a record address in the copy: with idx the two bits 29:28 of the shadow word at 132(sp), destination +0, the word at 288 + 4*idx and the address 896 + 16*idx; with idx the two bits 31:30, destination +16, the word at 272 + 4*idx, address 832 + 16*idx; with idx bits 27:26, destination +20, word 304 + 4*idx, address 960 + 16*idx; with idx bits 23:22, destination +32, word 336 + 4*idx, address 1088 + 16*idx (all offsets from sp). The edge-flag byte at +48 is 1 when bits 28:24 of the word at 368 + 4*idx (idx bits 19:18 of the word at 132) equal 0xf, else 0. Both paths then copy 40 material floats from context +0x590 to +0x62c (1424 to 1580) into +52 to +208 in order, word for word, except that the words at +1500 and +1496 go to +124 and +128 and the words at +1580 and +1576 go to +204 and +208 (two pairs swapped). If the byte at context +3220 (RGBA visual) is zero or bit 7 of +0x700 is clear the routine returns here. Otherwise it reads the colour-material face at +0x568 (1384): 0x404 or 0x408 selects the front-face copy first, 0x405 or 0x408 the back-face copy; any other value returns. The colour-material parameter at +0x56c (1388) then selects which four-float groups of the record take the current colour from +0 to +12: for the front face 0x1200 gives +52, 0x1602 gives +52 and +68, 0x1201 gives +68, 0x1202 gives +84, 0x1600 gives +100; for the back face 0x1200 gives +132, 0x1602 gives +132 and +148, 0x1201 gives +148, 0x1202 gives +164, 0x1600 gives +180; any other parameter copies nothing. A face of 0x408 does both copies, the front one first. The 0x408 test at 0xd970954 is always taken for any face other than 0x408, so a front-only face returns after its front copy.
- `__glOdysseyInitKernel` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c63c0, 656 bytes): **established.**
  Attaches a new context to the kernel driver. It looks along the ring of sharing contexts (next pointer at +0x16c70) for another context whose kernel-record pointer at +0x16c94 is set, and puts that context's word at +0x7b4c (or -1) into the request at +4. The request is eleven words: flags at +0 (bit 0 set when the environment variable `DECOUPLE_SWAPBUF` starts with `y`, `Y` or `1`), the shared word, this context's +0x7b4c, and the 32-bit address of the context's hardware-state block at +0x7b78. `__glOdysseyIoctl` request 0x6f640000 answers with fields that are stored at +0x16d3c (the slot word `__glOdysseyFinish` sends), +0x16d20 and +0x16d28 (the address of the hardware shadow, minus 0x900), +0x16c74, +0x16d2c, +0x16d30, +0x16d38 and +0x16c98 (the completion-slot pointer `__glOdysseyFinish` polls). A failed call returns 0, as does a zero value for +0x16c98. Otherwise it releases the old reference object at +0x16c78 through `__glOdysseyClosePMObjReference`, then either takes the sharer's kernel record (+0x16c94) and name array (+0x16414) and raises the array's count at +8, or allocates a 540-byte record with the host callback at +0 and a one-element name array with `__glNamesNewArray` (a failure sets GL_OUT_OF_MEMORY, 0x505, and returns 0), and returns 1.

## Pipe-memory object helpers

The decompiler names the calls of this group after the nearest exported symbol; the callee names here come from the address each call loads (the compilation unit's own static names).

- `CheckMMXfrArgs` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c6ca0, 128 bytes): **established.**
  Checks that a transfer request fits the buffer it names. With n the high half of the word at +0x28, w the low 20 bits of the word at +0x34 and s the word at +0x30, it returns 1 when the buffer length at +0x18 is at least s x (n - 1) + w x n, and 0 otherwise; n = 0 stands for 65536, for which the bound is s x 0xffff + (w shifted left 16).
- `__glOdysseyPMObjGroupResident` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c7170, 168 bytes): **established.**
  Reports whether a pipe-memory object range is resident on the board. It calls `LookupKnownGroup` (0xd9c7960) with creation off and `LookupCoresidRange` (0xd9c7af0) with creation off, and returns 0 when either finds nothing; otherwise it returns 1 when the halfword at the address held in the range record at +0x24 is nonzero.
- `__glOdysseyOpenPMObjReference` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c7220, 584 bytes): **established.**
  Opens a read reference to a run of words of a pipe-memory object and copies them out. Object number -3 stands for the kernel's attach record: it copies the words at +0x16d40, +0x16d68, +0x16d7c, +0x16d90 and +0x16da4 into destination words 0, 4, 5, 6 and 7, and -4 copies the word at +0x16d54 into word 0; in both cases, when the last argument is zero, it sends the settle sequence and then block `0x10658` with the operand `0x120000`. For any other number it calls `LookupKnownGroup` and `LookupCoresidRange` with creation on, returns 0 when either fails or when the range's busy word at +0x10 is already set, sets that word to 1, calls `OpenPipeMemReference` with mode 0 and copies words `lo` to `hi` inclusive of the group's data array (the pointer at +8) to the destination, and returns the result of `OpenPipeMemReference`.
- `__glOdysseyOpenPMObjWriteReference` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c7470, 564 bytes): **established.**
  Opens a write reference to one word of a pipe-memory object. Object number -3 copies one of the attach record's words selected by the index argument (0 selects +0x16d40, 4 selects +0x16d68, 5 +0x16d7c, 6 +0x16d90, 7 +0x16da4; other values copy nothing) and -4 copies the word at +0x16d54; as in the read form, a zero last argument sends the settle sequence and block `0x10658` with `0x120000`, and the routine returns 1. For other numbers it calls `LookupKnownGroup` with creation on and `LookupCoresidRangeWithLevel` (0xd9c7d30), returns 0 on failure or when the range's busy word at +0x14 is set, sets it to 1, calls `OpenPipeMemReference` with mode 1 and returns its result after storing the group's data word at the index into the destination.

## Pipe-memory object bookkeeping

Every context keeps the host's picture of the board memory objects it uses. Three structures, all in the context record: a ring of contexts that share one kernel attach record (next-pointer at +0x16c70), a "known group" set at +0x16c78 whose elements are the group records below, and a pointer at +0x16c94 to the attach record the kernel shares between those contexts, which holds a second set of the sharing contexts themselves. The sets are chunked sparse arrays ("c-sets"): a circular doubly linked list of nodes, each node a 16-byte header (table pointer at +0, number of used slots at +4, next at +8, previous at +0xc) over a table of 2^shift words, with the set header holding the list head at +0, a cursor node at +4, the node count at +8, the element count at +0xc, a cursor slot index at +0x10, a cursor-finished flag at +0x14 and the shift at +0x18. A group record is 0x54 bytes: its id at +0, the kernel's object handle halfword at +4, the object address at +8, sixteen list heads at +0xc to +0x4c (one list of range records per level count 0 to 15) and a word at +0x50. A range record is 0x2c bytes: next at +0, its group at +4, level at +8, covered count at +0xc, a read-busy flag at +0x10, a write-busy flag at +0x14, two words preset to -1 at +0x18 and +0x1c, a status-slot index at +0x20, a counter index at +0x22, a pointer to the 16-byte counter record at +0x24 and a pointer to a 16-bit status slot at +0x28. Object numbers -3 and -4 never have group records; they stand for the kernel's attach record (see `__glOdysseyOpenPMObjReference` above). Every routine below calls the context's allocator and release entries (allocate at +0, allocate-count-by-size at +4, release at +0xc) through the process-global context pointer or the context argument.

- `InitCSet` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c7f00, 20 bytes): **established.**
  Prepares an empty set (arguments: the set and a shift). It stores the shift at +0x18 and zeroes the head, node count and element count (+0, +8, +0xc); the cursor fields are left to `InitCSetCursor`.
- `InitCSetCursor` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c82d0, 20 bytes): **established.**
  Restarts a walk over a set: the finished flag (+0x14) and slot index (+0x10) become 0 and the cursor node (+4) becomes the list head (+0).
- `NewChunkNode` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c8070, 240 bytes): **established.**
  Makes one node for a set whose shift is the argument. It takes a 16-byte header and a table of 2^shift words from the context at the process address 0x1010, zeroes the table, zeroes the header's count, next and previous words and points the header's first word at the table. When either allocation fails it releases the one that succeeded and returns 0.
- `AllocSpecificCSetId` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c8160, 356 bytes): **established.**
  Stores a value at a chosen id of a set (arguments: set, id, value) and returns the id, or -1 when a node could not be made. The node number is the id shifted right by the set's shift; while the node count is not above that number it appends new nodes at the tail of the circular list (the first node points to itself). The table slot is the low bits of the id; the value is written without looking at what was there, then the node's used count and the set's element count are incremented, so storing twice at one id counts twice.
- `LookupCSetId` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c8470, 108 bytes): **established.**
  Reads the value stored at an id: it follows the next pointer (id shifted right by the shift) times from the head and returns the table word selected by the low bits, or 0 when the head is null or the list is shorter.
- `DeallocCSetId` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c8400, 104 bytes): **established.**
  Clears an id of a set: walks to its node as the lookup does, zeroes the table slot and decrements the node's used count and the set's element count. There is no check that the slot was in use.
- `NextCSetElem` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c82f0, 272 bytes): **established.**
  Returns the next stored value of a set in node order, skipping zero slots and nodes whose used count is 0, and keeps its place in the cursor fields (+4, +0x10, +0x14). When it passes the last slot of a node it moves to the next node and sets the finished flag when that node is the head again; it returns 0 once the flag is set, and also when the cursor node is null.
- `DestroyCSet` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c7f20, 308 bytes): **established.**
  Empties a set (arguments: the set and an optional per-element routine). For each of the set's nodes it calls the routine, when one is given, on every nonzero table word, releases the table and then the node through the context at 0x1010, and finally zeroes the shift, node count and head.
- `AddKnownGroup` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c7d90, 68 bytes): **established.**
  Registers a group record in a known-group set under the id held in the record's first word, with `AllocSpecificCSetId`, and returns nonzero when the id that call gave back differs from the record's own (a failure).
- `RemoveKnownGroup` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c7de0, 204 bytes): **established.**
  Forgets a group record (arguments: context, record). It releases every range record on each of the record's sixteen lists (+0xc to +0x4c), clears the record's id from the known-group set at the context's +0x16c78 with `DeallocCSetId`, releases the record and returns 1.
- `DestroyKnownGroup` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c7ec0, 60 bytes): **established.**
  The per-element routine `UnSharePM` hands to `DestroyCSet`: it calls `RemoveKnownGroup` (0xd9c7de0) with the context at the process address 0x1010 and the element. The generated listing labels this call `__glOdysseyClosePMObjReference`; the branch target in the plain disassembly is 0xd9c7de0.
- `LookupKnownGroup` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c7960, 388 bytes): **established.**
  Finds the group record for an object number (arguments: context, number, create flag). Numbers -3 and -4 return 0. Otherwise it looks the number up in the set at +0x16c78 and returns what is stored. If nothing is stored and the create flag is set it takes 0x54 bytes from the context, zeroes them, writes the number into the first word, registers the record with `AddKnownGroup` and creates the level-0 range record with `LookupCoresidRange` (level 0, count 15, create on); if that fails it removes the record again. An allocation failure raises GL error 0x505 (out of memory).
- `LookupCoresidRange` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c7af0, 568 bytes): **established.**
  Finds, or asks the kernel to describe, the range record of a group for a level and a count (arguments: context, group, level 0 to 15, count, create flag). It scans the group's list for that level and returns the first record whose covered count (+0xc) is at least the requested count. When there is none and the create flag is set, it takes a 0x2c-byte record, presets it (group and level stored, count stored, both busy flags 0, two words -1) and sends ioctl `0x6f640004` (the kernel's object query) with the flag word 2, the group id and the level and count halfwords. On success it keeps the reply: if the reply's flag word has bit 4 it stores one reply word at the context's +0x16c9c plus four times a reply index, which is how the context's table of block pointers (the first entry, +0x16c9c, names the block whose first two words the open and close routines use as an id table) receives a new block; it stores the object handle at the group's +4, computes the group's object address at +8 as the entry for the handle's high byte in the kernel record's table (kernel record at +0x16c94, table at +0x1c) plus 0x40 times the low byte, and takes group +0x50 from the reply; it stores the status-slot index (+0x20) and points +0x28 at that halfword of the shared status area whose base is the pointer at +0x16c98; it stores the counter index (+0x22), points +0x24 at the 16-byte record found by the index's low ten bits in the block named by its high six bits (blocks from +0x16c9c), copies the status halfword into that record's +4 and clears its +6 and +8; and links the record at the head of its list. On failure it releases the record and returns 0.
- `LookupCoresidRangeWithLevel` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c7d30, 92 bytes): **established.**
  Finds a range record that covers a level, looking first at the lists for that level and then at each lower one (arguments: context, group, level, create flag). A negative level goes straight to the next step; a record qualifies when its count at +0xc is at least the level. When nothing qualifies it branches to `LookupCoresidRange` (0xd9c7af0) through the always-taken `bgezl` at 0xd9c7d7c, with level and count both equal to the level. The load at 0xd9c7d74 puts that same address in t9 first, but the branch does not read t9.
- `OpenPipeMemReference` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c6fb0, 444 bytes): **established.**
  Marks a range as in use and makes sure the board holds it (arguments: context, group record, range record, extent, mode). When the mode is 0, the range was last opened in mode 1 (+0x1c) and the old extent (+0x18) plus the new one is positive, it first drains the pipe: `scripts/fifo_words.py` over 0xd9c6fb0 to 0xd9c716c lists 17 FIFO stores, all constants, namely the 15-word settle sequence (the settle-needed word at +0x9830 is cleared after its eleventh word) and then `0x10658` with `0x120000`. It then stores mode and extent at +0x1c and +0x18. When the group's word at +0x50 is 0 it stores the group's id in the first of the first two words of the block named by the pointer at +0x16c9c that still holds -1. It sets the counter record's +6 halfword to 1 and adds 1 to its +4 halfword, executes a memory barrier, and when the counter record's second halfword is nonzero or its first is zero it sends ioctl `0x6f640003` (the kernel's make-resident request) with the group id and the range's level and count halfwords; if that fails it undoes the counter changes and returns 0. It returns 1 otherwise.
- `__glOdysseyClosePMObjReference` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c7800, 352 bytes): **established.**
  Closes a read reference taken by `__glOdysseyOpenPMObjReference` (arguments: context, object number, level, count). Numbers -3 and -4 return 1 at once. Otherwise it finds the group (no create) and the range (no create), and when the range's read-busy word (+0x10) is set it clears it, clears the counter record's +6 halfword and decrements its +4 halfword, puts -1 back in whichever of the first two words of the block at +0x16c9c holds the group's id when the group's +0x50 is 0, and sends block `0x10a5a` with two operands, the range's halfword at +0x20 and the counter's +4 halfword. `scripts/fifo_words.py` over 0xd9c7800 to 0xd9c7960 lists those three stores and nothing else. It returns 0 when the group, the range or the busy flag is missing.
- `__glOdysseyClosePMObjWriteReference` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c76b0, 324 bytes): **established.**
  The same close for a write reference (arguments: context, object number, level). It finds the group without creating it, then the range with `LookupCoresidRangeWithLevel` (no create), clears the write-busy word (+0x14) when set, and does what the read close does with the counter and the id slot, ending with the same three-word `0x10a5a` block (`scripts/fifo_words.py`, 0xd9c76b0 to 0xd9c77f4, 3 stores).
- `__glOdysseySharePM` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c6650, 88 bytes): **established.**
  Puts the first context into the sharing ring of the second. When the second context's ring word (+0x16c70) is 0 it is first set to point at itself; the first context's word is then set to the second, and the walk from the second context along the ring to the last member (the one whose word points back at the second) makes that member point at the first, so the new context is appended.
- `__glOdysseyUnSharePM` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c66b0, 308 bytes): **established.**
  Takes a context out of its sharing ring and drops its use of the shared records. The ring predecessor is made to point at the context's own successor (when the word is nonzero), and the word is cleared. If the context has a kernel attach record (+0x16c94 nonzero) it empties the known-group set (+0x16c78) with `DestroyCSet`, giving it `DestroyKnownGroup`, decrements the use count at +8 of the shared record at +0x16414, removes the context's own id (+0x16c74) from the set in the attach record, and when the use count reaches 0 frees the shared names array through `__glNamesFreeArray`, empties the attach record's set without a per-element routine and releases the record. It clears +0x16c94.
- `__glOdysseyCreatePMObj` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c67f0, 508 bytes): **established.**
  Creates a board memory object (arguments: context, a ten-word description, a pointer to the object number, a halfword). It sends ioctl `0x6f640001` (the kernel's allocate request) with the number the caller holds as the old id, the halfword, and the ten description words. If the request fails, the id field the kernel wrote back is -1 and the caller still holds a number, it removes that number's group from every context in the kernel record's set (a walk with `InitCSetCursor`, `NextCSetElem`, `LookupKnownGroup` without create and `RemoveKnownGroup`), stores -1 as the number and returns 0. On success with a reply id other than -1 it stores that id as the number; when the reply flag word has bit 1 it also stores a reply word into the kernel record's table at +0x1c, indexed by a reply halfword; it returns 1. A reply of -1 returns 0.
- `__glOdysseyDestroyPMObjGroup` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c69f0, 344 bytes): **established.**
  Destroys a memory object together with its whole group (arguments: context, pointer to the object number). It first removes the number's group record from every context in the kernel record's set (the same walk as the create routine's failure path) and then sends ioctl `0x6f640002` (the kernel's release request) with the number; on success it stores -1 as the number and returns 1.
- `__glOdysseyDestroyPMObj` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c6b50, 324 bytes): **established.**
  Destroys one entry of an object's group (arguments: context, pointer to the object number, entry index 0 to 16). An index outside that range returns 0. It sends ioctl `0x6f640002` with the number, the index halfword and an "entry only" word of 1 in the request; if the kernel's reply clears that word (the whole group is gone) it removes the group record from every sharing context as `__glOdysseyDestroyPMObjGroup` does and stores -1 as the number. It returns 1 on a successful request.
- `__glOdysseyAssignToPMObj` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c6d20, 340 bytes): **established.**
  Sends host data to a memory object (arguments: context, group id, entry index, host address, length, flags, a caller word, pointer to the eight-word transfer header). It masks the header's word 5 to 17 bits in the caller's copy, builds the 80-byte request of `docs/kernel.md` (group at +4, entry halfword at +8 (stored by the `sh` at 0xd9c6d84), address at +0x10, length at +0x18, the header at +0x1c, flags at +0x3c with bit 0 forced on for a write, the caller word at +0x40), refuses with 0 when `CheckMMXfrArgs` says the buffer is too short, sends ioctl `0x6f640006`, and on success stores the reply's operation number at +0x21ee8 of the context, or 0 when bit 1 of the flags asked the kernel to wait. It returns 1 on success.
- `__glOdysseyReadFromPMObj` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9c6e80, 296 bytes): **established.**
  The same transfer for a read from the board: identical request layout and checks, but the flags are exactly what the caller passes (no bit 0), and the header is copied into the request and not modified except for the same 17-bit mask on word 5.

## Texture format class codes and the mipmap fill-in

Both format routines below are switch ladders over the driver's internal texture format index (0 to 49, the index space of `BUZZ.h`, "Texel formats"). The ladders were recovered with `ghidra/scripts/IrixJumpTables.java`, which finds each routine's 50-entry table of targets (0xdae944c and 0xdae9384); the index-to-case map was read from the table words in the file and the case bodies from the decompilation, and the first routine's answers for several indices and GL formats were also run in an emulator (Unicorn, the library's own code and tables, no board).

- `ChooseSuitableBuzzTexModeBaseFmt` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9cfc80, 320 bytes): **established.**
  Gives a small class code for a texture, from the internal format index (first argument) and the GL internal format the application named (second). Indices 0, 1 and 8 (the alpha formats) give 1; 4, 5 and 10 (luminance) give 2; 11 and 16 (luminance and alpha) give 3; 2, 3 and 9 (intensity) give 4; 6, 7 and 48 (3-3-2 and the 5-6-5 ICC format, no alpha) give 5; indices 14, 15, 19 to 31, 34 to 47 and everything from 50 up give 0. The seven formats that carry colour with room for alpha (12, 13, 17, 18, 32, 33 and 49) give 5 when the application's GL format is 3, GL_RGB (0x1907), GL_R3_G3_B2 (0x2a10) or any value from 0x804e to 0x8054 (GL_RGB2_EXT, then GL_RGB4 to GL_RGB16), and 6 for every other GL format, including 4, GL_RGBA and the sized RGBA formats. That the codes are the texture base-format classes (alpha, luminance, luminance-alpha, intensity, RGB, RGBA, in that numbering) is **inferred** from the formats that map to each; no consumer was traced.
- `GetImageFmtEnumFromBuzzTexFormat` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9cfbd0, 172 bytes): **established.**
  Converts a texel format index to the pixel class number the image code uses (`docs/pixel-paths.md`, the class list). The table has 16 hits: 0 gives 0, 2 gives 1, 4 gives 2, 8 gives 3, 9 gives 4, 10 gives 5, 11 gives 6, 12 gives 7, 13 gives 8, 16 gives 9, 17 gives 10, 18 gives 11, 33 gives 12, 32 gives 13, 48 gives 14 and 49 gives 15. Every other index (the odd members of the paired formats 1, 3 and 5, the 3-3-2 pair 6 and 7, 14, 15, 19 to 31, 34 to 47 and 50 up) gives -1. This is where the classes 0, 1, 3, 4 and 13 that only texture objects reach come from (A8, I8, A12, I12 and the 12-bit RGBA that index 32 names), and it shows that classes 14 and 15 are the two ICC texture formats.
- `ChooseSuitableBuzzTexObjFormat` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9cfdc0, 1040 bytes): **established.**
  Turns the GL internal format (first argument) into the board texel format index that `AllocateGfxMemForTexture` stores in the level record and `__glOdysseyTextureActivate` puts in bits 25:20 of the level descriptor. The body is a binary search over the enumerants 1 to 4, 0x1906 to 0x190a, 0x2a10, 0x803b to 0x805b, 0x8466 and 0x8467; every other value returns 0, which is also the answer for the alpha formats. It was read in full and run for every value from 0 to 0xffff in all eight builds; [texture-formats.md](texture-formats.md) tabulates the result. It returns 12 for `GL_R3_G3_B2`, `GL_RGB2_EXT`, `GL_RGB4`, `GL_RGBA2` and `GL_RGBA4`, and its set of results (0, 2, 4, 8 to 13, 16 to 18, 33, 48, 49) leaves out the odd members of the twin pairs, 6 and 7, and 32.
- `CalcTexelSizes` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9d01d0, 444 bytes): **established.**
  Reports the component widths of a texel format index (first argument) through six output pointers and a seventh output, the size code. It zeroes the six widths, then a 50-entry jump table (0xdae9514) sets them: indices 0 and 1 write 8 to the fourth output, 2 and 3 write 8 to the sixth, 4 and 5 write 8 to the fifth, 6 and 7 write 3, 3, 2 to the first three, 8 writes 12 to the fourth, 9 writes 12 to the sixth, 10 writes 12 to the fifth, 11 writes 8 to the fourth and fifth, 12 writes 4 to the first four, 13 writes 5, 5, 5 and 1, 16 writes 12 to the fourth and fifth and size code 1, 17 writes 8 to the first four and code 1, 18 writes 10, 10, 10 and 2 and code 1, 32 and 33 write 12 to the first four and code 2, 48 writes 8, 8, 8 and 49 writes 8 to the first four with code 1. Every other index leaves its widths zero and does not write the size code, so the caller's value stays (the sweep's `-` entries: 14, 15, 19 to 31, 34 to 47, 50 and 51). Indices 52 to 63 are not in the sweep: they rest on the index < 50 bounds check at 0xd9d01e4, which was read but not run. That the outputs are red, green, blue, alpha, luminance, intensity in that order is **inferred** from the GL formats that select each index; the size code is the `c` of the allocator's element size `2 << c` ([opengl.md](opengl.md#texture-level-records-and-memory)). Run for indices 0 to 51 in all eight builds.
- `CalcPixelModePixelFormat` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9d0390, 24 bytes): **established.**
  Maps the size code (first argument) to the pixel-format code that `AllocateGfxMemForTexture` calls it for (at 0xd9d061c) and stores in word 8 of the level record: the size code 1 (4-byte texels) gives 1 and every other code gives 0, which separates the 4-byte formats from the 2- and 8-byte ones. `DownsampleTextureThruHW` loads that word (0xd9d15e0, and again at 0xd9d1a68) into bits 9:7 of the pixel-mode word at context +0x96a4, the layout-code field of [pixel-paths.md](pixel-paths.md). What the board does with it is **unknown**.
- `__glOdysseyImageCalcTexObjFormats` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9beda0, 416 bytes): **established.**
  Gives the texel format index and a base class for a source image's pixel class (first argument, 0 to 17; 18 and above return 0 and write nothing), the two outputs being the index and the class. The pairs are the table in [texture-formats.md](texture-formats.md#who-writes-the-field); `__glOdysseyImageMovePixelsTexture` stores the index in bits 25:20 of level descriptor 60 and ignores the class.
- `DoMipmapHackHW` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9d1b30, 644 bytes): **established.**
  Builds the missing lower mipmap levels of a texture object on the board (arguments: context, texture object, first level, last level; result 1, or 0 after a failure). Level records are 0x44 bytes apart from the object's +0x568: state word at +0x568 (1 after the application supplied the level, 2 after this routine made it), the format word at +0x56c, the two extents at +0x570 and +0x574, a border word at +0x57c (so the word at +0x57c of the object itself is the border of level 0, which settles the open question of `SetTexOffsetValues`; **inferred** from its use here, where twice its value is taken from the level's extent before halving). It saves the user's raster state (`__glOdysseyImageSaveUserBuzzRasterState`) and loads the library's own (`__glOdysseyImageLoadBuzzRasterState` with 0). For each level above the first it halves both extents of the one below (each extent less twice its border, shifted right once, at least 1; a 1D target, 0xde0, takes a border of 0 for the second extent), adds the borders back and allocates board memory for the level with `AllocateGfxMemForTexture` (0xd9d03b0); a failed allocation puts the saved raster state back and returns 0. It then opens the object's board-memory reference over the level range (`__glOdysseyOpenPMObjReference` with the handle at +0x4fc, the object's table at +0x514 and the write flag 1), fills each new level with `DownsampleTextureThruHW` (0xd9d11a0; source level n, destination n + 1), stores state 2 in each new level record, closes the reference and restores the raster state. The generated listing labels every one of the static callees `__glOdysseyGetCurrentTexture`; the targets above are the addresses the instructions load.
- `DoMipmapHack` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9d1dc0, 452 bytes): **established.**
  Decides whether a texture object's lower levels have to be generated and, if so, branches to `DoMipmapHackHW` through the always-taken `bgezl` at 0xd9d1f4c (arguments: context, texture object; result 1 when nothing needed doing or on success). It returns 1 at once when the pending flag at +0x500 of the object is 0, and clears the flag otherwise. The last level to fill is the smaller of the object's last level (+0x60) and its level count (+0x9c); for a target other than 0x8095 it is also limited to the level count minus the context's word at +0x19ddc, and when that limit cuts the range it stores `((limit - base level) x 256) & 0x3fff` (0 when negative) in the object's maximum-LOD word at +0xcc and sets bit 14 of the dirty word at +0xa0. For target 0x8095 (GL_DETAIL_TEXTURE_2D_SGIS) the range is levels 0 to the level count with no limit and the object's base level is taken as 0. If the base level is not below the last level there is nothing to build. It then looks at levels base + 1 to last: if any of them has a positive extent and state 1 (the application loaded it) it zeroes +0xcc, sets bit 14 of +0xa0 and returns 1, leaving the application's levels alone. Otherwise, when a texture is bound (+0x16418), it deactivates it with `__glOdysseyTextureDeactivate` (0xd9d2540), and branches to `DoMipmapHackHW` (0xd9d1b30) with the object's base level and the last level, the delay slot restoring the stack.

## Bounding-box test for display lists

- `__glOdysseyIsBboxClippedRange` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd973750, 3496 bytes): **established** for the arithmetic, **inferred** for the names of the box fields and of the matrix terms.
  Returns 1 when a display list's bounding box cannot be skipped as inside the view, 0 when the box's whole projection is small enough (arguments: context, a nine-float box record). When the word at +0x16738 is set it hands over to `__glOdysseyIsBboxClippedRange2` and returns its answer. Otherwise there are three linear forms of a corner (x, y, z): w from the row at +0x95b8, +0x95bc, +0x95c0 (constant +0x95c4); a depth term from the matrix record at +0x7414 (x, y, z coefficients at +0xb8, +0xc8, +0xd8 and constant +0xe8, the third column of the 4 x 4 at +0xb0, the column `__glOdysseyValidateMatrixComb` above combines with the fourth); and two screen terms from the rows at +0x9588 (constant +0x9594) and +0x9598 (constant +0x95a4). The box floats 0 to 5 are x min, x max, y min, y max, z min and z max, in that order (the edge lengths are formed as max minus min). Phase one starts at the corner (x min, y min, z min), evaluates w and the depth term there, and visits the other seven corners along a Gray-code path, one edge per step (+x, +y, +z, -y, -x, +y, -z; corner numbers 0, 4, 6, 7, 5, 1, 3, 2 with bit 2 = x max, bit 1 = y max, bit 0 = z max), tracking the corner whose depth term over w is smallest by cross-multiplied comparisons (no division); with w negative the comparison is made on the negated values. If w has opposite signs at two of the corners it returns 1 straight away (the box straddles the eye plane). Phase two starts at the chosen corner and walks all eight corners again along the same path, with each edge step negated when the start corner is already at that axis's maximum. Each edge length is first limited: when the largest of the three floats at +0x18, +0x1c and +0x20 of the record (box floats 6 to 8) is not above the z edge length, all three edge lengths become that largest value (which of the box's fields these are was not traced; **inferred** to be a half-size or radius used as an upper bound). At each corner it forms the two screen terms and w and keeps, for each screen axis, the corners with the smallest and the largest screen term over w (cross-multiplied again). The result is 1 when either axis's extent (largest minus smallest screen term over w, now divided) exceeds the float at +0x19d94 times the constant 2.0 (0xdaf4b40), and 0 otherwise. In the normalised units of x over w that threshold is a multiple of the 2.0 width of the view, so the test says the box projects larger than that multiple of the viewport.

## Bitmaps

A bitmap is drawn as quads that use the board's polygon stipple, so the host never touches pixels: each 32 by 32 block of the bitmap is loaded as the 32-word stipple pattern (class-1 blocks 0x04e and 0x04f, 16 words each, from the context words +0x7b78 to +0x7bf4) and one rectangle of that size is drawn with polygon stipple enabled (bit 4 of the state word at +0x7bfc, the polygon-stipple enable that `__glOdyim_Enable` sets for GL_POLYGON_STIPPLE).

- `LoadAndSendStippledQuads` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd95f560, 2140 bytes): **established.**
  Draws a whole bitmap as a grid of stippled rectangles (arguments in order: context, the bitmap record, the raster z as a float, two texture coordinates as floats, a third as a double, the pointer to the bitmap's bytes; the record's words 0 and 1 are the bitmap's width and height in pixels, words 2 and 3 its origin offsets). The grid is ceil(width / 32) columns by ceil(height / 32) rows; the last column and row use the remainder width and height (the low five bits of the extents, or 32 when they are a multiple of 32). For each cell it packs the cell's bitmap bytes (ceil(cell width / 8) bytes per row, up to 4 per word) into the 32 words at +0x7b78 to +0x7bf4, filling them from the highest address down, then sends, in the order `scripts/fifo_words.py` prints them, the settle prefix `0x10443` 250 `0x10046` `0x10046` when the settle-needed word +0x9830 is set (clearing it); `0x1043f` with +0x7bf8; `0x13c4e` with the 16 words +0x7b78 to +0x7bb4; `0x13c4f` with the 16 words +0x7bb8 to +0x7bf4. It then draws the cell as one rectangle: `0x14400` with operand 7 (the quad primitive), a vertex word (`0x8f80a944` with no texture bound, `0x8f80b968` with one; in both bit 23 is set and bits 13:10 hold the operand count, 10 and 14, which is the 4 colour words and 6 position words plus, for the textured one, 4 texture-coordinate words), the four floats of the current raster colour from the record that +0xf8 points to, the corner (x, y, z) and the opposite corner (x + cell width, y minus cell height, z), then for the textured form the two texture coordinates twice, and `0x14001`. When the word +0x96f4 is nonzero it sends the settle prefix again and clears +0x9830; it sets +0x9830 to 1 at the end of the cell. x and y come from the raster position at +0x170 and +0x174 less the pixel-zoom origin words +0xe4c and +0xe50, plus the cell's offset less the record's origin words 2 and 3, with 1.0 added to y; the cell rows run downward in y. When a 3D texture is bound (target 0x806f) and the r coordinate argument is not zero, the rectangle is drawn instead through the context's own dispatch table (slot +0x740 of the table at +0x17f4, with the corners and r; what that slot is was not traced). `scripts/fifo_words.py` over 0xd95f560 to 0xd95fdbc lists 80 stores to the FIFO window in its 37 reachable blocks: the settle prefix at two sites, the stipple loads, the textured draw (a block of 14 operands) and the untextured one, and the closing `0x14001`.
- `DrawBitmapUsingPolyStipple` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd95fdc0, 2784 bytes): **established.**
  Wraps `LoadAndSendStippledQuads` in the state changes a stippled quad needs and undoes them (arguments: context, bitmap record). It returns at once when the byte at +0x165c4 is set. It makes the clip pair +0x7c20 and +0x7c24 equal to +0x165bc and +0x165c0, and when they differed sends block `0x1081d` with the two words, tells the kernel with ioctl 0x6f64000d the four 12-bit fields of them when bit 11 of the word at +0x7b70 is clear, sets bit 13 of the dirty word +0x16964 and the global 0x1018 to 2. It saves the 32 stipple words, +0x7bf8, +0x7bfc and +0x9828, updates the raster position when the byte at +0xa73c is set, and finds the raster position's eye-space z with `__glOdysseyImageCalcOdyEyeSpaceRasterPosZ`. With a texture bound it takes the current texture coordinates (+0x120, +0x124, +0x128, divided by q at +0x12c when that is not zero); if the third is nonzero and the texture is 3D it saves and replaces the projection, model-view and texture matrices and the current colour and texture coordinate through the context's own entry points (matrix mode enumerants 0x1700 to 0x1702; the projection becomes an orthographic one from 0 to +0xe74 across and 0 to +0xe78 up with near and far at -1.0 and 1.0, constants at 0xdaf4d70 and 0xdaf4d78), remembering the GL error state; which dispatch-table slot is which GL call is **inferred** from the arguments passed. It then sets bits 4:0 of +0x7bf8 to 4, clears bit 11 of +0x7bfc when lighting is on and bit 7 when bit 24 of +0x700 is set, sets bit 4 (polygon stipple on) and sends block `0x10404` with it; when the polygon modes at +0x1e4 and +0x1e8 are not both GL_FILL it saves +0x7c04, sets its low nibble to 10 and sends `0x10415`; when user clip planes are enabled (+0x70c) and the board has hardware clip planes it saves +0x7c00, clears its low three bits and sends `0x10405`; it clears the low 12 bits of +0x9828 and sends `0x10456`. After a validation (`__glOdysseyValidateHardware`) when the dirty word +0x16960 is nonzero it calls `LoadAndSendStippledQuads`, restores the saved words in the context and resends the stipple blocks (`0x1043f`, `0x13c4e`, `0x13c4f`), `0x10404`, `0x10415` and `0x10405` when it changed them, and `0x10456`, and finally undoes the 3D-texture matrix changes and the error state. `scripts/fifo_words.py` over 0xd95fdc0 to 0xd9608a0 lists 95 constant-address stores.
- `__glOdysseyRenderBitmap` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd9608a0, 232 bytes): **established.**
  Draws one bitmap in the current render mode and advances the raster position (arguments: context, bitmap record, draw flag). It does nothing when the raster-position-valid byte at +0x1c4 is 0. In GL_RENDER (0x1c00) it calls `DrawBitmapUsingPolyStipple` when the flag is nonzero, in GL_FEEDBACK (0x1c01) it calls `__glFeedbackBitmap` with the raster position record at +0xf0, and in GL_SELECT (0x1c02) it draws nothing. In every mode it adds the record's float at +0x10 to the raster x (+0x170) and the float at +0x14 times the signed integer at +0x12f4 to the raster y (+0x174).
- `__glOdyim_Bitmap` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd960990, 260 bytes): **established.**
  The glBitmap entry (arguments: width, height; the rest are passed on). With the dirty word +0x16964 zero it needs the global 0x1018 to be 0 or 2, otherwise 0x1018 may be anything but must be 0 or 2 too; any other value raises GL_INVALID_OPERATION (0x502) and returns. When the dirty word is nonzero or the global is 2 it sets the byte +0x16975, calls the context's validation entry at +0x2e40 and sets 0x1018 to 0. A negative width or height raises GL_INVALID_VALUE (0x501); otherwise it calls the bitmap routine at +0x3114 of the context with the two sizes and the remaining arguments.

## Enable, disable and enable queries

The three routines below are the board-specific halves of `glEnable`, `glDisable` and `glIsEnabled`: the generic library handles the enumerants the board has no state for, and these keep the host's picture of the enable bits that the board's state blocks are built from. All three read the context from the process address 0x1010 and refuse to run inside a glBegin and glEnd pair (the global at 0x1018 equals 1): they raise GL_INVALID_OPERATION (0x502) and return. An enumerant they do not know raises GL_INVALID_ENUM (0x500). The compiler turned each switch into a binary search, so no jump table is involved (`ghidra/scripts/IrixJumpTables.java` found none in either); the enumerant-to-bit tables below were read from the decompilation and then checked by running both routines in an emulator (Unicorn, the library's own code with a zeroed context, once with all state bits clear and once with all set so that cleared bits show; neighbours of each enumerant were probed to catch range tests). Enumerant names are from the public OpenGL registry. Unless a row says otherwise, a successful case also stores 2 in the global at 0x1018 ("state changed since the last validation"), which the next draw call turns into a validation. Words: +0x700 and +0x704 are the two main enable words, +0x708 the light enables, +0x70c the user clip-plane enables, +0x710 and +0x714 the map enables, +0x718 the asynchronous-operation flags, +0x7bfc the board's raster-control word (the first block `__glOdysseyValidateHardware` sends, `0x10404`), +0x16960 and +0x16964 the two dirty words.

- `__glOdyim_Enable` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd956bc0, 4920 bytes): **established.**
  Turns one capability on (argument: the enumerant). Each case sets one bit in an enable word and, where the board needs it, a bit in +0x7bfc and in the dirty words:
  GL_POINT_SMOOTH (0xb10): +0x700 bit 10, +0x7bfc bit 0, +0x16960 bit 1, +0x16964 bit 11 (bit numbers count from 0).
  GL_LINE_SMOOTH (0xb20): +0x700 bit 9, +0x7bfc bit 1, +0x16960 bit 1, +0x16964 bit 11.
  GL_LINE_STIPPLE (0xb24): +0x700 bit 8, +0x7bfc bit 3, +0x16960 bit 1.
  GL_POLYGON_SMOOTH (0xb41): +0x700 bit 11, +0x7bfc bit 2, +0x16960 bit 1, +0x16964 bit 11.
  GL_POLYGON_STIPPLE (0xb42): +0x700 bit 13, +0x7bfc bit 4, +0x16960 bit 1.
  GL_CULL_FACE (0xb44): +0x700 bit 12, +0x7bfc bit 8, +0x16960 bit 1, +0x2e24 bit 2 (a word of the context's dispatch state).
  GL_LIGHTING (0xb50): +0x700 bit 6, +0x16964 bits 6, 7 and 8; the global is set to 2.
  GL_COLOR_MATERIAL (0xb57): +0x700 bit 7, then `ValidateColorMaterial` (0xd9565c0) with the context; the global is not touched.
  GL_FOG (0xb60): +0x700 bit 5, +0x7bfc bit 22, +0x16960 bit 1.
  GL_DEPTH_TEST (0xb71): +0x700 bit 4, +0x7bfc bit 16 set from the byte at +0xc99 (nonzero when the visual has a depth buffer), +0x16960 bit 1, +0x16964 bit 11.
  GL_STENCIL_TEST (0xb90): +0x700 bit 15, +0x7bfc bit 15 set from the byte at +0xc9a (nonzero with a stencil buffer), +0x16960 bit 1, +0x16964 bit 11.
  GL_NORMALIZE (0xba1): +0x700 bit 22, +0x16964 bits 2 and 8.
  GL_ALPHA_TEST (0xbc0): +0x700 bit 0, +0x7bfc bit 14, +0x16960 bit 1, +0x16964 bit 11.
  GL_DITHER (0xbd0): +0x700 bit 3, +0x7bfc bit 18, +0x16960 bit 1.
  GL_BLEND (0xbe2): +0x700 bit 1, +0x16964 bit 11.
  GL_INDEX_LOGIC_OP (0xbf1): +0x700 bit 2, +0x7bfc bit 19, +0x16960 bit 1, +0x16964 bit 11.
  GL_COLOR_LOGIC_OP (0xbf2): +0x704 bit 11, +0x7bfc bit 20, +0x16960 bit 1, +0x16964 bit 11.
  GL_SCISSOR_TEST (0xc11): +0x700 bit 14, then the context's two scissor entries at +0x2ec4 and +0x2ebc are called with the context; the global is not touched.
  GL_TEXTURE_GEN_S, T, R and Q (0xc60 to 0xc63): +0x700 bits 18 to 21, +0x16964 bits 5 and 8.
  GL_AUTO_NORMAL (0xd80): +0x700 bit 23 only.
  GL_MAP1_COLOR_4 to GL_MAP1_VERTEX_4 (0xd90 to 0xd98): bit n of +0x710 for the n-th of the nine enumerants in that order; no other change.
  GL_MAP2_COLOR_4 to GL_MAP2_VERTEX_4 (0xdb0 to 0xdb8): the same in +0x714.
  GL_TEXTURE_1D (0xde0) and GL_TEXTURE_2D (0xde1): +0x700 bit 16 and bit 17, +0x16964 bits 4 and 7 to 10 (0x790).
  GL_POLYGON_OFFSET_POINT (0x2a01) and GL_POLYGON_OFFSET_LINE (0x2a02): +0x704 bit 9 and +0x7bfc bit 5, +0x704 bit 10 and +0x7bfc bit 6, each with +0x16960 bit 1.
  GL_POLYGON_OFFSET_FILL (0x8037): +0x700 bit 24, +0x7bfc bit 7, +0x16960 bit 1.
  GL_CLIP_PLANE0 to GL_CLIP_PLANE5 (0x3000 to 0x3005): bit n of +0x70c, +0x16964 bits 4, 8 and 10 (0x510).
  GL_LIGHT0 to GL_LIGHT7 (0x4000 to 0x4007): bit n of +0x708, +0x16964 bits 6 and 8 (0x140).
  GL_CONVOLUTION_1D, GL_CONVOLUTION_2D and GL_SEPARABLE_2D (0x8010 to 0x8012): +0x700 bits 25 to 27, and bit 4 of +0x2e24.
  GL_HISTOGRAM (0x8024) and GL_MINMAX (0x802e): +0x700 bits 28 and 29 only.
  GL_RESCALE_NORMAL (0x803a): +0x704 bit 16, +0x16964 bit 2.
  GL_TEXTURE_3D (0x806f): +0x700 bit 30, +0x16964 0x790 as for the 1D and 2D targets.
  GL_VERTEX_ARRAY, NORMAL_ARRAY, COLOR_ARRAY, INDEX_ARRAY, TEXTURE_COORD_ARRAY and EDGE_FLAG_ARRAY (0x8074 to 0x8079): a bit of +0x1528 (0x20, 0x01, 0x02, 0x08, 0x04 and 0x10 in that order) and a field mask in +0x151c (0xff, 0xf00, 0xff000, 0xf0000000, 0x7f00000 and 0x8000000); no global change.
  GL_INTERLACE_SGIX (0x8094): +0x704 bit 5.
  GL_TEXTURE_COLOR_TABLE_SGI (0x80bc): +0x7cd4 bit 0, +0x700 bit 31, +0x16960 bit 9.
  GL_COLOR_TABLE, GL_POST_CONVOLUTION_COLOR_TABLE and GL_POST_COLOR_MATRIX_COLOR_TABLE (0x80d0 to 0x80d2): +0x704 bits 0 to 2.
  GL_FOG_OFFSET_SGIX (0x8198): +0x704 bit 4, +0x16964 bit 12.
  GL_ASYNC_HISTOGRAM_SGIX (0x832c): +0x718 bit 3; GL_ASYNC_TEX_IMAGE_SGIX, GL_ASYNC_DRAW_PIXELS_SGIX and GL_ASYNC_READ_PIXELS_SGIX (0x835c to 0x835e): +0x718 bits 0 to 2; GL_PIXEL_TEXTURE_SGIS (0x8353): +0x704 bit 14; GL_INTERLACE_READ_INGR (0x8568): +0x704 bit 17.
  GL_VERTEX_PRECLIP_SGIX (0x83ee): when the byte at +0x19db4 is clear it sets that byte and the byte at +0x19dc0 to 1, sets +0x16964 bit 10, stores 2 in the global and calls `__glOdysseyFindWindowSize`; when it was already set it only calls `__glOdysseyFindWindowSize`.
  GL_FRAGMENT_LIGHTING_SGIX (0x8400): +0x704 bit 12, +0x16960 bit 2, +0x16964 bits 6, 7 and 8 (0x1c0); GL_FRAGMENT_COLOR_MATERIAL_SGIX (0x8401): +0x704 bit 13, +0x16964 bit 7; GL_FRAGMENT_LIGHT0_SGIX to GL_FRAGMENT_LIGHT7_SGIX (0x840c to 0x8413): bit n of +0x1647c, +0x16964 bits 6 and 8.
  Everything else raises GL_INVALID_ENUM. `scripts/fifo_words.py` over 0xd956bc0 to 0xd957ef8 lists no store to the FIFO window in its 260 reachable blocks, and neither does it for `__glOdyim_Disable` (0xd95dc70 to 0xd95f0f8, 271 blocks) or `__glOdyim_IsEnabled`: the routines only mark state and the board sees it at the next validation.
- `__glOdyim_Disable` (`builds/6.5.30-media/usr/gfx/arch/IP30ODSY32/libGLcore.so`, 0xd95dc70, 5256 bytes): **established.**
  The inverse of `__glOdyim_Enable` for the same enumerants, with the same dirty-word and global changes, each case clearing the bits the enable case sets in the enable words, +0x7bfc, +0x1528, +0x151c, +0x704, +0x708, +0x70c, +0x710, +0x714, +0x718 and +0x1647c (the cleared masks, for example 0xfffffbff in +0x700 and 0xfffffffe in +0x7bfc for GL_POINT_SMOOTH, were read from the second emulator pass). The differences are these. GL_LIGHTING, GL_SCISSOR_TEST, the texture-gen cases and GL_COLOR_MATERIAL behave as the enable case does with the bit cleared (GL_COLOR_MATERIAL again calls `ValidateColorMaterial`). GL_TEXTURE_3D also ANDs +0x7c00 with 0xfffff3df and sets +0x16960 bit 2. Disabling a texture-gen coordinate (0xc60 to 0xc63) clears its +0x700 bit, sets +0x16964 bits 5 and 8, and then toggles the GL_NORMALIZE bit (bit 22 of +0x700) twice: each time it validates through the context entry at +0x2e40 when the global is 2, flips the bit, sets +0x16964 bits 2 and 8 and stores 2 in the global, and it sets the byte at +0x16976 at the end; this makes the board's normalisation setting be rebuilt after a texgen change, and leaves the bit as it found it. GL_VERTEX_PRECLIP_SGIX clears the byte +0x19db4 only when it was set (and sets +0x19dc0, +0x16964 bit 10 and the global) and does nothing otherwise, without calling `__glOdysseyFindWindowSize`.
